# uopt.c Custom Hooks Reference

## Date: 2026-04-02 (last full revision); reviewed 2026-05-11

**Last reviewed: 2026-05-11**

All 19 documented `IDO52_*` hooks are present in
`tools/ido-static-recomp/build/5.2/uopt.c` (verified via `grep -oE
'getenv\("IDO52_[A-Z_]+"' uopt.c | sort -u`): `IDO52_SKIP_PASSES`, `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES`,
`IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES`, `IDO52_NO_FINDINDUCT_STRIDE`,
`IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE`, `IDO52_NO_CSE`, `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES`,
`IDO52_CSE_DEBUG`, `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS`, `IDO52_MAX_SREGS`,
`IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS`, `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG`, `IDO52_NO_LICM`,
`IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE`, `IDO52_STRENGTH_REDUCE_DEBUG`,
`IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS`, `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN`, `IDO52_NO_SREG_PROMOTE`,
`IDO52_DUMP_WEBS`. Added since: `IDO52_NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT` /
`IDO52_LOOP_TEST_REPL_DEBUG` (Hook 20, 2026-07-26) and
`IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_DEBUG` (Hook 3, 2026-07-26). The summary table below
covers most of these (a few — `NO_SREG_PROMOTE`, `DUMP_WEBS`,
`STRENGTH_REDUCE_DEBUG` — exist in the binary but were not given dedicated
narrative sections).
`SKIP_PASSES` now has a dedicated section (Hook 15, at the end of this file).

---

## CRITICAL WARNING

**`tools/ido-static-recomp/build/5.2/uopt.c` is NOT tracked in git.**

This file is auto-generated by `make setup-ido52` (which runs `ido-static-recomp`'s
recompilation process). Running `make clean` or `make setup-ido52` will **regenerate
the file from scratch**, destroying all custom hooks.

**Before running any clean/rebuild**: back up `uopt.c` manually.

**If hooks are lost**: use this document to re-implement them. The hook locations are
identified by the recompiled function names (e.g., `f_isearchloop`, `f_procinit_regs`),
which are stable across regenerations.

---

## Overview

All hooks are implemented as C code injected into the statically-recompiled IDO 5.2
`uopt` binary. The recompiled code simulates the original MIPS execution using C
variables for registers and a flat `uint8_t *mem` array for memory. Hooks are inserted
at specific points in the recompiled functions to alter optimizer behavior.

### Architecture

```
C source -> cfe (frontend) -> U-code -> uopt (optimizer) -> U-code -> upas/as1 -> object
                                          ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
                                          Hooks operate here
```

All hooks are:
- Gated by environment variables (parsed once, cached in static globals)
- Initialized lazily via `ido52_init_hooks()` (idempotent, called at first use)
- Declared at the top of `uopt.c` (lines 3-40) with the init function at the bottom (~line 221591)

### Build System Integration

The `@CompileEnv:` annotation in C source file headers sets environment variables
for that file's compilation. Parsed by `tools/build/parse_postproc.py`.

```c
/* @CompileEnv: IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32 IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=0 */
```

## ⭐ TREE-WIDE SWEEP RESULTS (2026-08-23) — read this before opening a new hook

Every hook below was built to close ONE function and then never swept across
the open population. Four sweeps in one investigation, each judged on the
**name-derived resolver** (`resolve_overlay_relocs.py`), never masked shape,
and each accepted only with **`len t == len c`** and a semcheck:

| family | settings tried | candidates | improved |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Hook 15 `IDO52_SKIP_PASSES` — all 15 subsets of {codemotion, eliminduct, opt_saved_regs, loopunroll} | 15 | ~120 | **20** |
| Hook 21 `IDO52_AS1_{NO,FORCE}_LA_PULL` — indices 0..15 + `all` | 34 | 95 | **39** |
| booleans/small values — `NO_LICM`, `NO_CSE`, `NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS`, `NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE`, `NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT`, `NO_FINDINDUCT_STRIDE`, `AS1_NO_PASS1_PROP`, `AS1_KEEP_ANDI`, `MAX_SREGS=0..4`, `LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1/4/1,4`, `CSE_MAX_REUSE=1/2` | 18 | 126 | **9** |
| Hook 27/35 `IDO52_AS1_XBB_SKIP_CAND` 0..11, `AS1_XBB_VOLATILE_OK` 0..9/`all`, `AS1_XBB_BUDGET` 0/1, `AS1_NO_FALLTHRU_FILL`, plus `NO_LICM_ADDRS`, `CMOT_KILL_AVAIL`, `RELOAD_SUPPRESS` | 26 | 126 | **31** |
| Hook 37 `IDO52_FORCE_WEB_COLOR` — every web `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` reports mapped (first 8) × v0/v1/s0-s7/t0-t5/fp | ~136 | 99 | **33** |
| Hook 36 `IDO52_UGEN_TEMP_REG` — every temp-register REQUEST `IDO52_UGEN_TEMP_REG_TRACE` reports (up to 24) × t0..t9 | ~240 | ~101 | **65** |

### ⭐ 2026-09-17: the FIRST blind Hook 36 sweep of the MAIN binary

The table above is an OVERLAY sweep. The main-binary open set had never had
one, and four of six functions in a batch that closed 2026-09-17 had never
been swept at all. All **215** open main-binary semis that are IDO (not
`@Compiler: gcc*`) and did not already carry the hook were swept; the result
is recorded in every one of those 215 headers, positive or negative
(`grep -l "HOOK 36 BLIND SWEEP" src/main/semi-matched/`).

| | n |
|---|---:|
| swept | 215 |
| **zero** temp-register requests — the hook has no site to act on | 26 |
| applicable | 189 |
| **improved** | **102 (54%)** |
| words gained | 713 |
| reached byte-perfect | **0** |

**The 65% figure does not hold on the main binary; 54% does.** Biggest:
`func_80052A80` +66, `_sprite_set_box_shade` +29, `func_8004C50C` +34,
`func_8008EB8C` +24, `basyo_flag_set` +22, `message_disp_switch` +17.

Three things the earlier write-up does not say:

1. **Hook 36 fixes COUNTS, not just register names.** Eight functions reached
   their target word count outright under a force — `func_8004C50C` 142→143,
   `func_800AA914` 151→152, `func_800ACB5C` 170→171, `func_8008F790` 37→39,
   `func_8008E660` 91→92, `func_800A1414` 53→54, `func_800A97B0` 147→148,
   `func_800AE9F0` 166→168 — three of them also dropping harness Fixup words
   to 0. A forced free-list pop can remove a spill/reload word, so **do not
   skip the sweep because the function is count-wrong**; that is a reason to
   run it.
2. ⚠️ **`Exact: X/N` from `test_single_match` uses `N = min(target, compiled)`,
   so a force that SHORTENS the body raises the score.** Six first-pass "wins"
   were exactly that. Guard every main-binary hook sweep on `Target`,
   `Compiled`, `Body` and `Fixup` from the same run, and accept a shape change
   only when the body grows TOWARD the target's own word count, never past it
   and never away. This is the main-binary form of the overlay
   `len t == len c` rule.
3. **Sweep the free list, not a t0..t9 grid.** `IDO52_UGEN_TEMP_REG_TRACE=1`
   prints the whole free list at each request; forcing the head is the default
   pop, so probing `free[1:]` makes every probe a force that genuinely fires.
   A no-gain result is then a statement about the hook's *choices*, not about
   the grid that was tried — which is what makes the 87 recorded negatives
   worth having.

**It closes nothing on its own, and the reason is structural.** The near-100%
semis are precisely the ones it does not move: they have been ground over, and
what is left is const-web formation, base-register CSE and as1 delay-slot
fills. Worked proof on `event_log_add` (90/102, the closest): the residual
needs the write-index increment in `$t6`, `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` shows the value is
the body's one coloured web, and a Hook 37 force of that web over all 26
register names beats nothing — with `t6/t7/t8/t9` refused outright by the
legality gate, because they have no colour at all. Hook 36 cannot reach it
either: a uopt web never asks ugen's free list. That is **web FORMATION**, the
wanted primitive recorded at Hook 37's boundary note, and it is now measured
on two specimens (`event_log_add`, `line_set`) rather than asserted.

Composing the as1 / SKIP_PASSES families on top of the eight newly count-exact
functions adds 3 words on `func_8004C50C` and 2 on `func_8008E660` and nothing
elsewhere — finding 2 below (the hooks compose) holds, but weakly here.

Four findings that change how to use these hooks:

1. **Sweep Hook 36 `UGEN_TEMP_REG` FIRST — it hits 65% of open functions.**
   `IDO52_UGEN_TEMP_REG_TRACE=1` prints `[UGEN-TREG36] occ=N free=[...]` for
   every integer temp-register request; force each occurrence to each of
   t0..t9. ugen's round-robin free-list pop diverges from the original build
   constantly, and ONE forced pop usually realigns a whole region
   (`ENDING/func_80137BE4` 95 → 137 on `2:t6`). Hook 37 `FORCE_WEB_COLOR` is
   second at 33% (`IDO52_DUMP_WEBS=1`, then each mapped web × 17 registers,
   and a SECOND web on top of the first is worth another pass). Both hooks had
   exactly one or two hand-diagnosed consumers before this; sweeping their
   parameter space blind beats reasoning about which web or occurrence to
   force. The XBB family is third at 25% — reach for it when the residual is a
   delay slot, a duplicated instruction, or a branch target off by one word.
   ⚠️ Hooks 36 and 37 both reshuffle register allocation, so an existing paint
   layer can silently become unsound: verify `audit_paint_isomorphism` reports
   **0 producer changes**, not just the resolver score.
2. **The hooks COMPOSE, they do not overlap.** `KANGEI/func_80133008` went
   7 → 218 (SKIP_PASSES pair) → 247 (LA_PULL index 12) → 275 (XBB_VOLATILE_OK=1)
   across three separate sweeps. **Re-run earlier sweeps after a new family
   lands.**
3. **Token PAIRS are not the union of their parts** — and this is per-hook.
   `SKIP_PASSES=eliminduct,loopunroll` scored 72 on `TACO/func_80153B08` where
   the parts scored 41 and 49; `codemotion,eliminduct` scored 218 on
   `KANGEI/func_80133008` where each part alone scored ~7. Both single-token
   sweeps had missed these entirely. Conversely, no LA_PULL index PAIR ever beat
   the best single index. Measure, do not assume.
4. **`NO_LA_PULL` won 30 of 39** — our as1 speculatively pulls `%hi` *more* than
   the retail as1 did far more often than it pulls less.

⚠️ **Guard every sweep on `len t == len c`.** Three Hook-15 "winners"
(`TACO/func_8013603C`, `TT/func_8013B4A0`, `TT/func_8014C2E8`) scored higher
only because the compiled body got SHORTER and the resolver compares to the
shorter of the two. A sweep without that guard manufactures fake progress.

⚠️ A strict `'clean' in semcheck_output` accept-test falsely rejects files with
**pre-existing** warnings (`TT/func_8013B6C8` carries two `$v0` load-use
hazards in its baseline object). Compare the warning SET, not its emptiness.

**Still unswept, in rough order of promise:** `IDO52_FORCE_WEB_COLOR`
(web:reg — needs a per-function `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` first), `IDO52_UGEN_TEMP_REG`
(occurrence:reg), `IDO52_CONST_INREG_VALUES` / `CONST_NOINREG_VALUES`,
`IDO52_AS1_TIE_SRC_ORDER`, `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB`, `IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS_WEB`.

## Hook 46: IDO52_AS1_FORCE_BOPT — f_do_branch_opt mips1 legality override (2026-08-23)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `IDO52_AS1_FORCE_BOPT` | `N[,N…]` (0-based candidate index) | `IDO52_AS1_FORCE_BOPT=3` |
| `IDO52_AS1_BOPT_TRACE` | presence | `[AS1-BOPT] #N bb=… tgt=… insn=…` |

### What it is for

Retail's as1 sometimes **copies** the branch target's first word into a delay
slot and **keeps the original**, bumping the branch displacement by one. Ours
**moves** instead. Two independent workers named that as the missing primitive
on 2026-08-23 (`EVENT/func_80138040`, `SHOUGATU/func_80133768`,
`get_h_tokimeki`, `step34_mainff`).

Reading `f_do_branch_opt` shows the copy-and-bump machinery **already exists and
is mips1-capable** — the applier's old note that "mips1 code never reaches its
commit path" is wrong:

```
L40a9c4:  t8 = MEM_S16(sp + 82);        /* branch displacement */
          t7 = MEM_U8(s2 + 0);          /* mips ISA level      */
          t9 = t8 + 1;
          if (t7 < 2) { MEM_U16(sp+82) = t9; goto L40aa4c; }   /* mips1: bump only */
          ...                                                   /* mips2+: beq->beql */
```
and the copy itself is `MEM_U32(t0 + -4) = t1` a few lines earlier. What differs
by ISA is only the **acceptance test**: at `L40a838` mips2+ jumps to
`v0 = 1` unconditionally (the likely form only executes the copy when the branch
is taken, so it needs no proof), while mips1 must pass `f_defuse` /
`f_cannot_trap` / the register-interference test, because a mips1 copy runs on
**both** paths.

This hook forces that mips1 verdict for one occurrence, at `L40a8f8`. The
emission is entirely as1's own committed code.

⚠️ **NOT automatically sound.** It overrides the test that proves the copied
instruction is safe when the branch is not taken. Byte-verify every use.

### Measured result: NEGATIVE so far, and it relocates the problem

Forcing **every** candidate on 12 near-miss functions changed **nothing**:

| function | candidates | base | best forced |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| TT/func_80137A80 | 5 | 101 | 101 |
| RPG_BAT/func_80156064 | 11 | 198 | 198 |
| TAIIKU/func_80145C44 | 7 | 98 | 98 |
| ENDING/func_8013858C | 7 | 135 | 135 |
| SHOUGATU/func_80133768 | 1 | — | — |
| ETC/func_801434BC, SHOUGATU/func_80139DD8, RPG_BAT/func_80142DFC, EVENT/func_80138040, DATE/func_80139E40 | **0** | | |

**Five of the twelve have no candidate at all**, including two of the three the
workers named. So the wall is NOT "as1 proposes the copy and rejects it" — it is
"as1 never proposes it". The gate is upstream of the legality test, in
`f_find_branch_target` / `f_eligible` / the `MEM_U16(v0+78)` and
`MEM_U32(t9+4)` block-shape tests at the top of the loop. That is where the
next attempt should go, and Hook 46 is the instrument that proves it: with the
legality test out of the way, the candidate list is still empty.

Verified inert when unset: 24 randomly sampled `byte_perfect` functions all
still byte-perfect after the as1 rebuild.

### Hook 46b: IDO52_AS1_NO_BOPT — the VETO direction (2026-09-17)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `IDO52_AS1_NO_BOPT` | `N[,N…]` / `occ:N[,N…]` (0-based candidate index) / `1` / `all` | `IDO52_AS1_NO_BOPT=occ:0` |

Same decision site (`f_do_branch_opt`'s final verdict at `L40a8f8`), opposite
direction: it sets `v0 = 0` for the named candidates, sending them down as1's
**own** reject path (`goto L40aab4`) — the path every candidate that fails
`f_defuse` / `f_cannot_trap` / the register-interference test already takes.
Unlike `FORCE_BOPT` it is **sound by construction**: a vetoed candidate is an
outcome as1 itself produces, and nothing is synthesised.

It exists because `IDO52_AS1_NO_BRANCH_OPT=1` is all-or-nothing (it returns
from `f_do_branch_opt` at entry) and a function often needs **some** of its
candidates. `main/CdControlF` (0x80095D00) is the first consumer: candidate #0
copies the else block's `lui a0,0xf000` into a `beqz` delay slot where retail
has a `nop`, while candidate #1 copies the join block's `lw ra,20(sp)` into the
following `b` delay slot exactly as retail does. `NO_BRANCH_OPT=1` killed both
and left a `nop` where retail has that `lw ra`; `NO_BOPT=occ:0` closed the
function byte-perfect 36/36.

Enumerate the index space with `IDO52_AS1_BOPT_TRACE=1` (same index space as
`FORCE_BOPT`). Applied by `apply_as1_delay_hooks.py`; rebuild with
`make VERSION=5.2`. Verified inert when unset: **195/195** sampled
`src/main/matched` objects bit-identical to the pre-hook as1, plus 5 objects
compiled with their own `@CompileEnv` as1 hooks applied.

### Hook 46c: the "as1 cannot copy-and-bump" claim is FALSE — read this before asking for one (2026-09-20)

**Two file headers independently asked to BUILD an as1 "copy the branch
target's first instruction into the delay slot and retarget past the original"
primitive, calling it the as1 twin of gccwrap's `--ido-copy-and-bump`. That
primitive is `f_do_branch_opt`, it already exists, and Hooks 46/46b already
expose it.** Both requests are withdrawn; do not build it a third time.

Evidence it is live, not theoretical: `IDO52_AS1_BOPT_TRACE=1` prints
candidates in **14 of 40 sampled `src/main/matched` files**, and `CdControlF`
(above) was closed byte-perfect by steering it.

**The retail shape is common.** Scanning the whole main binary for a
conditional branch `B -> T` where `word(B+4) == word(T-4) != 0` and `word(T-12)`
is an unconditional transfer (so `word(T-4)` is provably unreachable) gives
**449 dead-duplicate sites in 158 functions — 248 of them inside 69 still-open
semis**, plus 72 functions already `matched_c`. When a function is one word
short at a branch, this is worth checking before anything else.

**The two ways it fails, both diagnosed 2026-09-20 with an instrumented as1:**

| failure | symptom | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| **no candidate at all** (`main/func_8008FD70`) | `BOPT_TRACE` prints NOTHING | every candidate bails at gate 1, `MEM_U8(bb+70) & 1`. That flags2 bit is set by the branch classifier in `func_41d7b4` (opcode switch, sets it for op 4/5/6/7 = beq/bne/blez/bgtz). In that TU the classifier is reached 3 times and sees `jr ra` every time, so the `bne` block is never classified. |
| **candidates, but the wrong ones** (`TAIIKU/func_8013BF3C`) | `BOPT_TRACE` prints 6, all `eligible=1` | branch-opt is already doing all it can: `FORCE_BOPT` at occurrences 0..5 and `all` changes nothing (candidates already pass), `NO_BOPT` makes it worse (111 / 103 / 103 vs 113). The word retail copies (`andi t4,v0,0xf`) is simply never offered — a candidate-SELECTION problem in `f_do_branch_opt` / `f_find_branch_target`. |

⚠️ **`BOPT_TRACE` silence means MISSING CANDIDATE, not inactive pass.** An older
`NO_BOPT=occ:0` refutation on `func_8008FD70` was recorded against a pass that
never got a candidate there — it tested nothing.

**A gate-1 widening hook was prototyped and deliberately NOT shipped.** Letting
`f_do_branch_opt` consider a block whose flags2 bit 0 is clear does work
mechanically: on `func_8008FD70` it reaches exactly the right candidate
(`reached L40a838 eligible=1 w0=2402ffff`, and `2402ffff` IS the `li v0,-1`
retail copies). But the block only becomes available when the XBB boost is
suppressed, and that reshapes the body — measured masked scores 7/8 (skip 0),
8/9 (skip 0,1), 12/13 (skip 0,1,2), 7 (skip 0,1,2,3+) against a **baseline of
65**. Shipping it would add a hook that fires and closes nothing, which is the
inert-hook trap this file already documents. The narrower unmet need is a way
to keep the delay slot EMPTY at `f_do_branch_opt` time *without* suppressing
the XBB boost — in as1's ordering (`f_schedule` → `f_do_xbb_opt` →
`func_41d7b4` → `f_do_branch_opt`, re-confirmed by trace) those are the same
decision.


## Hook 1: IDO52_NO_CSE — Disable Common Subexpression Elimination

⛔ **2026-08-30 CORRECTION: this hook was a SILENT NO-OP from the day it was
written until `apply_uopt_readphase_hooks.py` (Hook 46) landed.**
`apply_uopt_hooks.py` calls `ido52_init_hooks()` inside `oneproc`, AFTER the
procedure's U-code has been read, while `f_isearchloop` (this hook's site) and
`f_isearchloop`'s callers `checkstatoccur` / `exprimage` run during the read.
The env was parsed too late; `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG` never printed a single
expression.  Proof: `A=x*y; if(X) B=x*y+2; C=x*y+3;` — one `multu` with
`IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES=mpy` before the fix, three after.  **Every "NO_CSE /
NO_CSE_OPCODES inert" refutation dated before 2026-08-30 tested nothing** (the
two listed below included).  One shipped user, `TAIIKU/func_80148458`, carried
an inert `IDO52_NO_CSE=1` that broke the match (11/45) once live; it was
retired.  Env-off output is unchanged (all 668 DATE functions re-resolved,
every shipped hook user re-checked).

### Status: PRESENT (restored 2026-04-03)

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_NO_CSE` | `all` or `op` | `IDO52_NO_CSE=op` |
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES` | comma-separated opcode names or numbers | `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES=shl,mpy,add` |
| `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Disables CSE in uopt's expression hash table. When active, forces creation of new
"ichain" entries instead of reusing existing ones. This prevents IDO from merging
identical subexpressions into a single register, producing separate computations
for each occurrence.

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_isearchloop` (expression hash search loop)
**Line**: ~94246 (after hash bucket head `s0 = MEM_U32(t9 + 0)`, before null check)
**Mechanism**: Sets `s0 = 0` to skip the hash search entirely, falling through to
`f_appendichain` which creates a new ichain entry.

### Modes

| Mode | Value | Behavior |
|------|-------|----------|
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_OFF` (0) | default | No effect |
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_OP` (1) | `op` or `1` | Skip CSE for operator expressions only (type 4=binary_op, 5=unary_op, 6=ixa) |
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_ALL` (2) | `all` | Skip CSE for ALL expression types |
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES` (3) | via `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES` | Skip CSE only for specified U-code opcodes |
| `IDO52_NO_CSE_TYPES` (4) | unused | Skip CSE for specified expression types |

### Parameter Mapping

In `f_isearchloop(mem, sp, a0, a1, a2, a3)`:
- `a0`: hash table entry pointer
- `a1`: expression descriptor pointer (type at offset +0, opcode at offset +32)
- `a2`: unknown (passed through)
- `a3`: unknown (passed through)
- `s0`: hash bucket head pointer (set to 0 to skip search)

Expression descriptor fields:
- `MEM_U8(a1 + 0)`: expression type (1=const, 2=var, 3=LDA, 4=binop, 5=unop, 6=IXA)
- `MEM_U8(a1 + 32)`: U-code opcode (for type 4/5 expressions)

### Available Opcode Names (25 total)

| Name | Value | U-code | MIPS equivalent |
|------|-------|--------|-----------------|
| `abs` | 0 | Uabs | absolute value |
| `add` | 1 | Uadd | addu/addiu |
| `and` | 4 | Uand | and/andi |
| `cvt` | 24 | Ucvt | type conversion |
| `cvtl` | 25 | Ucvtl | long conversion |
| `dec` | 26 | Udec | decrement |
| `div` | 29 | Udiv | div |
| `inc` | 55 | Uinc | increment |
| `ior` | 60 | Uior | or/ori |
| `ixa` | 65 | Uixa | indexed address |
| `lnot` | 80 | Ulnot | logical not |
| `max` | 85 | Umax | max |
| `min` | 86 | Umin | min |
| `mod` | 87 | Umod | modulo |
| `mpy` | 91 | Umpy | mult/multu |
| `mus` | 93 | Umus | multiply-sub |
| `neg` | 94 | Uneg | negate |
| `neq` | 95 | Uneq | not-equal |
| `not` | 97 | Unot | bitwise not |
| `rem` | 105 | Urem | remainder |
| `shl` | 115 | Ushl | sll |
| `shr` | 116 | Ushr | srl/sra |
| `sqr` | 118 | Usqr | square |
| `sub` | 125 | Usub | subu |
| `xor` | 141 | Uxor | xor/xori |

Numeric values also accepted: `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES=91,115` is equivalent to `mpy,shl`.

### Global Variables

```c
static int ido52_no_cse_mode;                          /* line 94222 */
static uint8_t ido52_no_cse_opcode_list[64];           /* line 94224 */
static int ido52_no_cse_opcode_count;                  /* line 94225 */
static uint8_t ido52_no_cse_type_list[16];             /* line 94227 */
static int ido52_no_cse_type_count;                    /* line 94228 */
static int ido52_cse_debug;                            /* line 94229 */
```

### Known Issues

- Blunt instrument: disabling CSE for all adds may help one expression but hurt others
- Cannot target a specific occurrence of an expression; affects all expressions matching the type/opcode -- **use Hook 1b `IDO52_NO_CSE_SITE=OPC:OCC` (2026-09-25) for one occurrence**
- `IDO52_NO_CSE=op` does NOT affect constant or variable loads (types 1-3), only operators
- Per-opcode mode only applies to binary_op (type 4) and unary_op (type 5), not IXA (type 6)

### Functions That Reference This Hook

**WIP** (tried but not matching):
- `scroll_poly_disp` — tested `IDO52_NO_CSE=add/shl/shr` and `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES=add,shl,shr`, no effect
- `grp_table_calc` — tested `IDO52_NO_CSE` (all ops), no effect on s-reg count

### Related Documentation

- the CSE hook investigation and its per-opcode results (kept in the project's private archive)

## Hook 2: IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES — Block Constants from Register Allocation

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES` | comma-separated decimal/hex values | `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32` |

### What It Does

Prevents specific constant values from being kept in registers across their lifetime.
When IDO decides whether a constant value is worth keeping in a register (to avoid
reloading it), this hook intercepts and returns "no" for the specified values. This
forces IDO to re-materialize the constant each time it's needed (via `li` / `lui+ori`).

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_constinreg` (decides if a constant should stay in a register)
**Line**: ~192068 (after prologue, before the function's decision logic)
**Mechanism**: If `a2` (the constant value) matches any entry in the blocked list,
writes 0 to the return slot at `sp+39` and jumps to the function's return path
at `L4853ac`.

### Parameter Mapping

In `f_constinreg(mem, sp, a0, a1, a2, a3)`:
- `a0`: stored to `sp+40` (context pointer)
- `a1`: register class/number (stored as byte at `sp+43`, also used in bitmask checks)
- `a2`: **the constant value** (the value being considered for register allocation)
- `a3`: stored to `sp+52` (additional context)
- Return value: `MEM_U8(sp + 39)` — 1 = keep in register, 0 = don't keep

### Global Variables

```c
static uint32_t ido52_const_noinreg_values[64];        /* line 94230 */
static int ido52_const_noinreg_count;                  /* line 94231 */
```

### Known Issues

- Return path uses the simulated stack (`MEM_U8(sp + 39)` then `goto L4853ac`) rather
  than a C `return`, which is required to preserve the caller's simulated register state.
- **melt_font_glyph_unpack regression**: Previously matched with `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32`,
  but when uopt.c was regenerated and hooks were re-implemented, the function now compiles
  to 112 instructions instead of the target 108. The original hook implementation was lost,
  and the re-implementation may differ in subtle ways (e.g., exact injection point, stack
  offset handling). See "Known Regressions" section below.

### Functions That Use This Hook

**Matched** (with regression):
- `melt_font_glyph_unpack` — `@CompileEnv: IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32` (produces 112 insns, target is 108)

**WIP** (tried but not matching):
- `callendar_disp_init` — tried `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=8`, did not help
- `get_fnt` — tried, could not disable LSR for the target loop
- `icon_atari_check` — best result 101 insns with `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=15`
- `normal_date_bg_out` — tried, did not help
- `func_800A04E0` — tried `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=28`, did not help
- `magazine_init` — noted as potential candidate

## Hook 3: IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES — Block Address Loads from Register Allocation

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES` | `CLASS[:FROM[:TO]][,...]` | `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1:3:7` |
| `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Prevents LDA (Load Address) expressions of specific "classes" from being kept in
registers. When IDO decides whether an address value (e.g., `&global_var`) should
stay in a register, this hook intercepts and returns 0 for blocked classes, forcing
IDO to re-compute the address each time.

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_ldainreg` (decides if an address should stay in a register)
**Line**: ~192267 (at function entry, before the function's decision logic)
**Mechanism**: If `a0` (the storage class) matches any entry in the blocked list,
immediately returns `v0 = 0` (don't keep in register).

### Parameter Mapping

In `f_ldainreg(mem, sp, a0, a1, a2)`:
- `a0`: **storage class** of the LDA expression (the value being checked)
- `a1`: stored to `sp+4` (context)
- `a2`: stored to `sp+8` (additional context, compared against 4 in decision logic)
- Return value: `v0` — nonzero = keep in register, 0 = don't keep

### Global Variables

```c
static uint32_t ido52_lda_noinreg_classes[64];
static int ido52_lda_noinreg_from[64];
static int ido52_lda_noinreg_to[64];
static int ido52_lda_noinreg_seen[64];
static int ido52_lda_noinreg_count;
static int ido52_lda_noinreg_debug;
```

### Occurrence windows (2026-07-26)

Each list entry may carry a 1-based occurrence window over `f_ldainreg`
queries **of that class** (mirrors the Hook 2 `CONST_NOINREG` window syntax).
A plain `CLASS` is `1..INT_MAX` — the historical block-everything behaviour,
fully backward compatible. `CLASS:0:0` blocks nothing and is the trace-only
form. `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_DEBUG=1` prints one line per query of a listed class:

```
[LDA-NOINREG] class=1 n=7 a1=0xffffffe0 a2=0x00000000 block=0
```

Every entry whose class matches advances its **own** counter on every query,
and all entries of the same class therefore see the same `n`. That makes an
arbitrary occurrence SET expressible as a list of singleton windows:

```
IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1:3:3,1:6:6,1:7:7,1:11:11    /* block {3,6,7,11} */
```

Occurrence numbering here is **stable** under blocking (unlike Hook 2's
`CONST_NOINREG`, which feedback-renumbers): the query stream is the ucode node
walk, which blocking does not reshape. Verified on TAIIKU/func_8013EE38 — every
single-occurrence probe `1:N:N` for N=1..28 produced a byte-identical object.
It is *not* stable across source or other-flag changes; re-derive after any edit.

### ⚠️ What the window can and cannot do (measured 2026-07-26)

`f_ldainreg` is asked once per LDA **node**, but the promotion bit is created
on the first unblocked query and reused by every later node of the same address.
Consequences, both confirmed on TAIIKU/func_8013EE38 and func_8013E3D4:

- **Blocking a single occurrence is a no-op.** A later query of the same
  address just creates the bit instead. All 28 singleton probes on
  `func_8013EE38` and all 28 on `func_8013E3D4` produced zero change.
- **The window's real granularity is the address (`a1`), not the use site.**
  Group the debug lines by `a1` and block a whole group to de-register that
  address. On `func_8013EE38` the 28 class-1 queries group into 10 addresses,
  of which only two are register-promoted at all (`a1=0xffffffe0` → +6 insns
  when blocked, `a1=0xfffffff0` → +3; together they are the whole +9 that the
  un-windowed `CLASSES=1` costs). The other eight groups are inert.
- **It cannot express a web SPLIT.** When one address has several uses and the
  target keeps *some* in callee-saved registers and rematerializes the rest,
  no window reproduces that: the hook's predicate is all-or-nothing per address.
  This is the shape of both TAIIKU residuals — see the Hook 20 section for the
  full negative result.

### Known Issues

- The `a0` parameter is a storage class code, not an address. The exact mapping of
  class values to source-level concepts is not fully documented. Class 0 appears to
  be static/global storage.
- Uses a direct C `return v0` (not simulated stack return), which may differ from the
  original hook implementation.

### Functions That Use This Hook

**Matched**:
- `melt_font_glyph_unpack` — `@CompileEnv: IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1` (combined with CONST_NOINREG).
  Class 1 = stack/auto storage. Prevents `bits` array base address from being kept
  in a dedicated register, saving 1 instruction.

**WIP** (tried but not matching):
- `callendar_disp_init` — tried, did not help
- `get_fnt` — tried, did not help
- `func_800A04E0` — tried, did not help
- `magazine_init` — noted as potential candidate

## Hook 4: IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS — Reserve Specific S-Registers

### Status: PRESENT (restored 2026-04-03) — BUT INEFFECTIVE (see below)

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS` | comma-separated register names | `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS=s0,s1` |

### What It Does

ORs reserved register bits into `dftregsused` bitvector at `0x1001e608+4`.
Also re-applies the mask at the allocator entry point (`func_46c178` at ~line 155625).

### CRITICAL FINDING: `dftregsused` is OUTPUT, not INPUT

**The `dftregsused` bitvector at `0x1001e608` does NOT control s-register allocation.**
Investigation on 2026-04-02 proved:
- Setting ALL s-register bits (`MAX_SREGS=0`, mask `0x0003fe00`) still produces code using s1, s4, s5, s6
- The mask persists through the allocator (verified via debug output at `func_46c178` entry)
- The bitvector is an OUTPUT bitmap tracking which registers the function USES, not an INPUT controlling availability
- The actual register pool for Chaitin graph coloring is determined by architecture tables internal to the allocator

**The existing `-Wo,-no_r23` flag** reduces s7 USER variable assignment but doesn't prevent s7 save/restore entirely. It operates through a different mechanism (likely the flag byte at `0x1001eb10`).

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_procinit_regs` (line ~75763) AND `func_46c178` (line ~155625)
**Mechanism**: ORs mask into `MEM_U32(0x1001e608 + 4)`
**Effect**: None observable on s-register allocation

### Parameter Mapping

The hook operates on a global, not on function parameters:
- `0x1001e608`: address of `dftregsused` bitvector
- `MEM_U32(0x1001e608 + 4)`: high word containing s-register bits

### S-Register Bit Mapping

| Register | High-word bit |
|----------|---------------|
| `s0` | `0x00020000` |
| `s1` | `0x00010000` |
| `s2` | `0x00008000` |
| `s3` | `0x00004000` |
| `s4` | `0x00002000` |
| `s5` | `0x00001000` |
| `s6` | `0x00000800` |
| `s7` | `0x00000400` |
| `fp` | `0x00000200` |

### Global Variables

```c
static uint32_t ido52_reserve_sregs_mask_hi;           /* line 5 */
```

### Known Issues

- **Cannot reduce the total s-register save count.** IDO saves a contiguous range
  s0..sN in the prologue. Reserving s0 shifts allocation to s1, but the save count
  stays the same (s0..sN is still saved because s1..sN+1 are used, and saves must
  include all lower-numbered s-regs).
- Only shifts which s-registers are assigned, not how many. For reducing the count,
  use `IDO52_MAX_SREGS` instead (but that has its own limitations).

---

## Hook 5: IDO52_MAX_SREGS — Cap S-Register Allocation Count

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_MAX_SREGS` | integer 0-8 | `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=3` |

### What It Does

Caps the total number of s-registers available to the allocator. Setting `N` reserves
s(N) through s7 and fp, allowing only s0 through s(N-1). This forces more values to
spill to the stack.

### Where It's Hooked

Same location as `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS` — uses the same mechanism (ORing bits into
`dftregsused` high word at `0x1001e608 + 4`).

**Function**: `f_procinit_regs`
**Line**: ~76200 (shared with RESERVE_SREGS)

### Parameter Details

Setting `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=N` reserves registers s(N) through fp:
- `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=0` — reserves s0-s7 + fp (no s-regs available)
- `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=3` — reserves s3-s7 + fp (only s0-s2 available)
- `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=8` — reserves only fp (s0-s7 available, default-like)

### Known Issues

- Combined effect with `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS`: both OR into the same mask, so they
  can be used together.
- With `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=0`, all values spill to stack, producing significantly larger
  frames and instruction counts.
- Changing s-reg availability affects frame size (more/fewer saves in prologue/epilogue).

### Functions That Reference This Hook

**WIP** (tried but not matching):
- `sndi` — `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=0` gets correct frame size (280) and 0 s-regs,
  but instruction count is 262 vs target 258
- `grp_table_calc` — `IDO52_MAX_SREGS=3` produces 282 insns with 10 saves (spills everywhere)

---

## Hook 6: IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS — Protect Global Stores from Dead Store Elimination

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS` | comma-separated hex addresses | `IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS=0x800EF6EC,0x800EF6E8` |
| `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Prevents IDO from eliminating "dead" stores to specific global variable addresses.
IDO's optimizer detects when a store to a global is overwritten before being read
and eliminates the first store. This hook preserves such stores for specified addresses.

### Where It's Hooked

Two injection sites in two different optimization passes:

**Site 1 — Copy Propagation Pass**:
- **Function**: `f_copypropagate`
- **Line**: ~25780
- **Context**: After `f_bvectin` returns nonzero (store is considered dead), checks
  if the store's target address is protected. If protected, jumps to `L419580`
  (skipping the elimination).
- **Expression pointer**: `MEM_U32(s2 + 4)` — the expression being considered for elimination

**Site 2 — Induction Variable Elimination Pass**:
- **Function**: `f_eliminduct`
- **Line**: ~185566
- **Context**: Similar dead store elimination check within the induction variable pass.
  If the store's target address is protected, jumps to `L481694` (skipping elimination).
- **Expression pointer**: `MEM_U32(s0 + 4)` — the expression being considered

### Address Resolution

The helper function `ido52_is_protected_deadstore(mem, expr_ptr)` resolves the
store's target address from the expression descriptor:
- `expr_ptr + 0x28`: offset to `Expression.isvar_issvar.location.addr` — the
  runtime address of the global variable being stored to

### Global Variables

```c
static uint32_t ido52_no_deadstore_addrs[64];          /* line 14 */
static int ido52_no_deadstore_count;                   /* line 15 */
static int ido52_deadstore_debug;                      /* line 16 */
```

### Known Issues

- Maximum 64 protected addresses (`IDO52_MAX_DEADSTORE_ADDRS`).
- Only protects stores to globals at known fixed addresses. Dynamic/stack stores
  are not affected.
- Debug mode (`IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG=1`) prints to stderr for every store checked,
  which can be very verbose.

### Functions That Use This Hook

No matched or WIP functions currently use this hook via `@CompileEnv`. It was
implemented as an "open door" for ~4 functions with dead store elimination blockers
identified in the fourth investigation.

---

## Hook 7: IDO52_CSE_DEBUG — CSE Debug Tracing

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Enables debug tracing for the CSE hash table search. Prints expression type, opcode,
and hash bucket pointer to stderr for each expression entering `f_isearchloop`.

### Where It's Hooked

Same location as `IDO52_NO_CSE` — inside `f_isearchloop` at line ~94268.

### Output Format

```
[CSE] type=4 opc=91 (0x5b) s0=268813552
```

### Known Issues

- Very verbose for large functions (every expression in every basic block is logged).
- Useful for diagnosing which expressions are being CSE-merged.

## Hook 8: IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG — Dead Store Debug Tracing

### Environment Variable

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Enables debug tracing for dead store elimination. Prints the address of each store
being considered for elimination, and whether it was protected.

### Where It's Hooked

In `ido52_is_protected_deadstore()` helper function (line ~21) and in the init
function (line ~221722) for listing protected addresses at startup.

### Output Format

```
[DEADSTORE] checking store to addr=0x800ef6ec
[DEADSTORE] PROTECTED: skipping elimination of store to 0x800ef6ec
[DEADSTORE] protecting 2 addresses: 0x800ef6ec 0x800ef6e8
```

## Known Regressions

### melt_font_glyph_unpack (432 bytes, 108 instructions)

**Status**: UNRESOLVED. Confirmed unmatchable from C. File remains in
`src/main/semi-matched/melt_font_glyph_unpack.c`. Matched via `asm/main/matched/func_8004F918.s`.

**History**: Originally claimed matched with `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32` and
`IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1`. After uopt.c was regenerated and hooks were verified
working (debug prints confirmed both hooks trigger), the compiled output is still
112 instructions regardless of hook combinations tried:
  - No hooks: 112 insns
  - `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=32`: 112 insns (hook fires but no effect on count)
  - `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1`: 112 insns (hook fires 3x but no effect)
  - Both hooks combined: 112 insns
  - `IDO52_NO_CSE=all/op`: 112 insns
  - All CSE opcode combinations: 112 insns

**Root Cause**: The 112 compiled vs 108 target gap is structural — the cross-prologue
function entry passes `t6 = a0<<3` which IDO's body uses for `addu t6,t6,a0; sll
t6,t6,1; addu a2,t6,t7` (computing src address in a2 using t6 from cross-prologue).
The compiled version assigns different registers (v0 for src, t7/t9 for D_800BD250)
producing 3 extra instructions from register spills and different addressing. These
differences are fundamental to how IDO's register allocator responds to the cross-
prologue input, and no hook combination produces the target's exact register scheme.

**Lessons**: The previous "RESOLVED" note was aspirational — it documented what the
hooks SHOULD have done, not what they actually achieved. The function was never in
`src/main/matched/` and the custom_script uses `fix_remaining_reg_mismatches()` which
does direct instruction-level patching (allowed for register field changes only, but
cannot fix the 3-instruction count gap). The function is confirmed unmatchable.

---

## Initialization Function Reference

### `ido52_init_hooks()` (line ~221591)

Called lazily from each hook site. Parses all environment variables once on first call.

**Parsing order**:
1. `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG` — presence check
2. `IDO52_NO_CSE` — `"all"` or `"op"`/`"1"`
3. `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES` — comma-separated names/numbers, parsed via `_opc_names[]` table
4. `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES` — comma-separated decimal/hex values via `strtoul`
5. `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES` — comma-separated decimal/hex values via `strtoul`
6. `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS` — comma-separated register names via `_sreg_map[]` table
7. `IDO52_MAX_SREGS` — single integer 0-8, converted to reserve mask for s(N)..fp
8. `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG` — presence check
9. `IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS` — comma-separated hex addresses via `strtoul`

**Buffer sizes**: 256 bytes for most env vars, 512 bytes for deadstore addresses.

---

## Hook 10: IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE — CSE Freshness Counter

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE` | integer >= 1 | `IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE=1` |
| `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_TYPES` | comma-separated type numbers (1-8) | `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_TYPES=3,4` |
| `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Limits how many times an expression's ichain can be reused before forcing a fresh
computation. After N reuses of the same ichain (matched via hash table), the hook
forces a cache miss (`t0 = 0, s0 = 0`), causing `f_appendichain` to create a new
ichain entry. This prevents the optimizer from sharing a single register for repeated
identical subexpressions.

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_isearchloop` at label `L447124` (the convergence point where
match/no-match is decided, before success path L4479ac or append path).

**Mechanism**: Maintains an external hash map (`ido52_cse_track[]`, 4096 entries,
open-addressing) keyed on ichain pointer. On each hash hit, increments the reuse
counter for that ichain. If `count >= max_reuse`, sets `t0 = 0` (no match) and
`s0 = 0` (clear chain pointer), forcing creation of a new ichain.

### Expression Types

| Type | Name | Description |
|------|------|-------------|
| 1 | const | Constant values |
| 2 | var | Variable references |
| 3 | LDA | Load-address (address-of globals, locals) |
| 4 | binop | Binary operations (add, mul, shl, etc.) |
| 5 | unop | Unary operations (neg, not, cvt) |
| 6 | IXA | Indexed address (array indexing) |
| 7 | error | (unused) |
| 8 | extended | Extended expressions |

### Global Variables

```c
static int ido52_cse_max_reuse;                         /* 0 = disabled */
static int ido52_cse_freshness_debug;
static int ido52_cse_freshness_types[8];                /* type filter */
static int ido52_cse_freshness_type_count;              /* 0 = all types */

#define CSE_TRACK_SIZE 4096
static struct { uint32_t ichain_ptr; int count; } ido52_cse_track[CSE_TRACK_SIZE];
```

### Test Results (2026-04-01)

| Function | Baseline | With Hook | Target | Notes |
|----------|----------|-----------|--------|-------|
| func_80090DA8 | 138 | 142 (all,max=1) | 181 | +4 from address CSE; main gap is strength reduction, not hash-table CSE |
| touzyou_fan_disp | 1153 | 1156 (type=4,max=1), 1201 (all,max=1) | 1168 | type=4 gives +3; all types overshoots |
| don_move | 85 | 88 (all,max=1) | 86 | Overshoots; cast CSE not in hash table |
| func_80097AB4 | 172 | 176 (all,max=2) | 176 | COUNT MATCHES but structure differs: hidden t6/t7 regs + s0 frame blocker; unmatchable |
| normal_date_bg_out | 36 | 40 (all,max=3) | 40 | COUNT MATCHES but `lw t3,24(sp)` where target has `li v0,1`; constant type=1 not in CSE hash table; forced LDA misses change interference graph causing allocator spill; 47.5% with scheduler_normalizer; unmatchable |
| func_8004BF08 | 359 | 377 (all,max=4) or (type=3,max=4) | 377 | COUNT MATCHES but frame 208 vs 184 (+24=6 extra arg-home slots); compiled 47 lw vs target 25 lw; opcode-positional match 19.7%; scheduler_normalizer 26.3% but b-insn misplacement; unmatchable — hook forces too much spilling |

### Known Limitations

- **Strength reduction is NOT CSE**: The `voice*168` pattern in func_80090DA8 and
  similar functions is handled by `f_eliminduct` (induction variable optimization),
  not the CSE hash table. This hook cannot affect it.
- **Too blunt without type filter**: `max_reuse=1` with all types breaks too many
  CSEs. Always use `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_TYPES` to target specific expression types.
- **Cannot target specific variables**: The hook tracks ichain pointers, not variable
  names. All expressions of the filtered type are affected equally.
- **Counter persists across basic blocks**: The reuse count accumulates across the
  entire function, not per basic block.
- **Constants not in CSE hash table**: Constant expressions (type=1) are NOT processed
  through `f_isearchloop`. They are handled by `f_constinreg` instead, so
  `CSE_MAX_REUSE` cannot force constant recomputation. When forced LDA misses change
  register pressure, the allocator may spill constants to stack (`lw` instead of `li`).

---

## Summary Table

| # | Env Variable | Hook Function | Line | IDO Pass | Effect | Status |
|---|-------------|---------------|------|----------|--------|--------|
| 1 | `IDO52_NO_CSE` | `f_isearchloop` | ~94566 | CSE (expression hash) | Skip hash lookup, force new ichain | **PRESENT** (restored 2026-04-03) |
| 2 | `IDO52_NO_CSE_OPCODES` | `f_isearchloop` | ~94566 | CSE (expression hash) | Per-opcode CSE disable | **PRESENT** |
| 3 | `IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES` | `f_constinreg` | ~192388 | Register allocation | Block constant values from registers | **PRESENT** |
| 4 | `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES` | `f_ldainreg` | ~192574 | Register allocation | Block address loads from registers | **PRESENT** |
| 5 | `IDO52_RESERVE_SREGS` | `f_procinit_regs` | ~76072 | Register init | Reserve specific s-registers | **PRESENT** (restored 2026-04-03) |
| 6 | `IDO52_MAX_SREGS` | `f_procinit_regs` | ~76072 | Register init | Cap s-register count | **PRESENT** |
| 7 | `IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS` | `f_copypropagate` | ~26107 | Copy propagation | Protect stores from elimination | **PRESENT** (restored 2026-04-03) |
|   |                              | `f_eliminduct` | ~185882 | Induction elimination | (second site, same hook) | **PRESENT** |
| 8 | `IDO52_CSE_DEBUG` | `f_isearchloop` | ~94566 | CSE (expression hash) | Debug tracing | **PRESENT** |
| 9 | `IDO52_DEADSTORE_DEBUG` | `ido52_is_protected_deadstore` | ~header | Dead store elim | Debug tracing | **PRESENT** |
| 10 | `IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE` | `f_isearchloop` | L447124 | CSE (expression hash) | Limit expression reuse count | **PRESENT** |
| 11 | `IDO52_NO_LICM` | `func_47efd4` | ~182197 | LICM pass | Prevent loop-invariant address hoisting | **PRESENT** |
| 12 | `IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE` | `f_eliminduct` | ~185330 | Induction elimination | Skip func_47fbbc call | **PRESENT** |
| 13 | `IDO52_NO_FINDINDUCT_STRIDE` | `f_findinduct` | ~65889 | Induction analysis | Skip stride creation (func_431cfc) | **NEW** (2026-04-03) |
| 14 | `IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS` | `f_globalcolor` | ~157445 | Register coloring | OR exclusion mask into forbidden bitvector | **EXTENDED** (2026-05-09) |
| 14b | `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN` | `f_makelivranges` | ~127361 | Live range construction | Pre-color variable to specific physical register | **EXTENDED** (2026-05-09) |
| 15 | `IDO52_SKIP_PASSES` | driver function | ~126430+ | Multiple | Skip entire optimizer passes | **PRESENT** — all 7 tokens wired 2026-07-26 (see Hook 15 §) |
