## Hook 11: IDO52_NO_LICM — Disable Loop-Invariant Code Motion

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_NO_LICM` | `all` or comma-separated hex addresses | `IDO52_NO_LICM=0x800CFC5C,0x800CFF14` |

### What It Does

Prevents IDO's loop-invariant code motion (LICM) pass from hoisting address-of
computations out of loops into s-registers. When active, repeated global address
loads remain inside the loop rather than being promoted to a dedicated register
held across iterations.

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: Unnamed LICM hoist-marking function (injected before its prologue)
**Line**: ~181865 in current uopt.c
**Mechanism**: Hook is at the entry of the function that marks expressions as
loop-invariant. When `ido52_no_licm == 1` (all mode), the function immediately
returns, preventing all LICM marking. When `ido52_no_licm == 2` (address mode,
via `IDO52_NO_LICM_ADDRS`), checks for LDA expression type and compares address
against protected list; if matched, returns early skipping the hoist.

**Restoration point**: Find the `//nop; //nop; //nop;` block at ~line 181862,
immediately followed by `/* LICM disable hook */` comment, then inject the hook
before `sp = sp + 0xffffffe0` (function prologue).

### Known Issues

- `IDO52_NO_LICM=all` is very aggressive — prevents ALL LICM, often making output
  worse or unchanged (e.g., grp_table_calc: 289→296 insns with `all`).
- Per-address mode (`IDO52_NO_LICM_ADDRS`) was tested on game-region semi-matched
  functions (2026-04-01) and did not reduce instruction count (addresses
  0x80129C30, 0x800F1150, 0x800BA86C all unchanged at 191/192/283 insns).
- `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES` (Hook 4) prevents the promoted address from being kept
  in a register but does NOT prevent the LICM hoist itself. This hook targets the hoist.

### Functions That May Benefit

- `system_config_pad_mode` — IDO hoists &D_800CFC5C, &D_800CFF14 to s-regs
- `func_80063B78` — similar address hoisting pattern

### Tested Results (2026-04-01)

- `grp_table_calc`: `IDO52_NO_LICM=all` → 296 insns (worse); per-address → 289 (no change)
- `func_8005970C`: `IDO52_NO_LICM=all` → 192 insns (no change); per-address → 192 (no change)
- `save_format_main`: `IDO52_NO_LICM=all` → 168 insns (no change)

---

## Hook 14: IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS — Exclude registers from globalcolor

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS` | comma-separated reg names | `IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS=v0,t6` |

### What It Does

ORs the supplied register exclusion mask into both the per-web `forbidden`
bitvector (at `MEM_U32(s5+40)` during `f_globalcolor`) AND the global
allocator forbidden mask at `0x10001264`. Forces the Chaitin allocator to
skip those colors for any web being colored at that point.

**Supported registers** (extended 2026-05-09):
- Caller-saved: `v0`, `v1`, `a0`, `a1`, `a2`, `a3`, `t0`..`t9`
- Callee-saved: `s0`..`s7`, `fp`

Originally only callee-saved (s/fp) were supported. Caller-saved support
was added when matching work revealed many functions where IDO reuses a caller-
saved register the target binary doesn't.

### Usage

```c
/* @CompileEnv: IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS=v0 */
```

### Limitations

The exclusion applies during `f_globalcolor` only. It cannot change:
- The function parameter convention (a0 still receives the first param)
- Pre-coloring decisions made earlier in the pipeline (use
  `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN` for that)
- Register class restrictions enforced elsewhere

If IDO must allocate a register and only the excluded one fits the
constraints, the exclusion is silently ignored.

---

## Hook 14b: IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN — Pre-color variables to physical registers

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN` | `REG:BITPOS[,REG:BITPOS,...]` | `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN=v1:0` |

### What It Does

Forces specific variables (identified by their `bittab` index) to be
pre-colored to specific physical registers as Live-On-Entry. Useful for
matching functions where the original C compiled with a hidden parameter
in a non-ABI register (commonly v0/v1/t6 for dispatcher tables).

**Supported register colors** (RegisterColor enum — CORRECTED 2026-08-13
by empirical N-token sweep on RPG_BAT/func_80151600; the old t6..t9 rows
were fiction):

| Reg | Color | Reg | Color | Reg | Color |
|-----|-------|-----|-------|-----|-------|
| v0  | 1     | v1  | 2     | a0  | 3     |
| a1  | 4     | a2  | 5     | a3  | 6     |
| t0  | 7     | t1  | 8     | t2  | 9     |
| t3  | 10    | t4  | 11    | t5  | 12    |

Color 13 is the compiler's special $ra color; 14+ are invalid (allocator
ignores the pin).  **t6-t9 are NOT in globalcolor's pool** — they are
ugen scratch temps assigned after coloring, so a variable can never be
LIVEIN-pinned to them.  For hidden-t6 dispatcher params: pin the nearest
colorable register (v0..t5) and close the residual with rs-field paints
(precedent: RPG_BAT/func_80151600).  The parser now refuses t6..t9
tokens with a loud [PRECOLOR] warning; raw `N<color>` tokens (e.g.
`N13:2`) are accepted for probing.

### Usage

Requires knowing the variable's `bittab` index. Use `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` to
discover indices for a given function, then pre-color the desired
variable:

```c
/* @CompileEnv: IDO52_DUMP_WEBS=1 IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN=v1:3 */
```

### `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` — bitpos discovery (segfault FIXED 2026-07-26)

`IDO52_DUMP_WEBS=1` prints one line per populated `bittab` slot after
`f_globalcolor`; `=2` additionally prints the raw slot words. The printed
index IS the bitpos that `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB` / `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN`
take.

**Historical bug (fixed):** the dump used to kill `uopt` with
`Signal 11` partway through — "prints 14 of 170 webs then dies" — which
blocked PRECOLOR discovery on exactly the big functions that need it. Two
independent defects:

1. **`numwebs` (`0x1001c8a4`) is not the populated-slot count.** The tail of
   the `bittab` array holds stale/uninitialised words; a slot whose second
   word is a small integer (`0x33`, `0x13`, `0x183` …) was dereferenced as a
   liverange pointer. `libc_impl` reserves the whole arena `PROT_NONE` and
   biases `mem` by `-MEM_REGION_START`, so that read faults instead of
   returning garbage. This is why the crash looked size-independent: it fired
   only when a junk slot happened to fall *below* `numwebs`
   (BUNKASAI/`func_8013D8E8` crashed at slot 128 of 139; `func_801580F4`
   survived because its junk slots sat above 170).
2. **A silent `i < 200` cap** truncated the dump on large procedures — the
   webs PRECOLOR discovery needs were simply never printed.

The fix (in `apply_uopt_hooks.py`) probes every address with
`ido52_mem_readable()` before dereferencing it — a real touch with
`SIGSEGV`/`SIGBUS` redirected to a `siglongjmp`, handlers saved and restored
around each probe — and removes the cap. Unreadable slots print
`NOT-A-LIVERANGE (unmapped, skip)` and the walk continues; a run now ends
with `[DUMP-WEBS] N printed, M unmapped-slot(s) skipped`.

⚠️ Do **not** "simplify" the probe to a `write(2)` to `/dev/null` — the
kernel discards those writes without ever touching the user buffer, so every
address reports readable and the crash comes straight back. `mincore`/`msync`
are also wrong here: the `PROT_NONE` guard pages are still *mapped*.

The probe needs `sigaction`/`sigsetjmp`, which `-std=c11` hides, so the hook
script also injects `#define _POSIX_C_SOURCE 200809L` ahead of
`#include "header.h"`. Everything is inside the env-gated dump path:
with `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS` unset the compiled objects are byte-identical
(verified by md5 A/B on five overlay functions against the pre-fix `uopt`).

### `IDO52_UOPT_TRACE` — needs a listing file

`IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5` (and every other level) drives the *original* IDO dump
routines, which write to the listing file opened by the undocumented `-l`
flag. Without it they write through a NULL `FILE*` and `uopt` dies with
`Signal 11` — this is a usage error, not the `DUMP_WEBS` bug. Pass it
through `cc`:

```bash
IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5 cc -O2 -G0 -mips1 -EL -non_shared -Wo,-l,trace.lst -c f.c -o f.o
```

The hook now prints a reminder to stderr whenever the variable is set.

### Limitations

- Only Live-On-Entry coloring; doesn't affect mid-function variables.
- Bitpos discovery requires running with `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS=1` first.
- Multiple entries supported (max 8).
- **CAVEAT (2026-05-09)**: The post-globalcolor hook updates the web's
  recorded color and rebinds the global ichain table at `gh+0x44+...`,
  but does NOT re-emit the function body. Affected instructions may
  still reference the original register choice. Verified test:
  `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN=v0:0` reports `assigned_reg 4->1` but the
  emitted `slti at, a0, 6` still uses `$a0`. To get full register
  rebind from C, combine with `IDO52_EXCLUDE_REGS` for the original
  register, or use `swap_registers` / `prepend_thunk` PostProc.

---

## Hook 12: IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE — Disable Strength Reduction in f_eliminduct

### Status: PRESENT (restored 2026-04-03)

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE` | `1` | `IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE=1` |
| `IDO52_STRENGTH_REDUCE_DEBUG` | any value | `IDO52_STRENGTH_REDUCE_DEBUG=1` |

### What It Does

Prevents `f_eliminduct` from applying strength reduction transformations. Specifically
skips the `func_47fbbc` call that replaces induction variable expressions with
accumulative versions at the U-code IR level.

### Where It's Hooked

**Function**: `f_eliminduct` (induction variable elimination)
**Line**: ~185005 (before `func_47fbbc` call in the bitvector iteration loop)
**Mechanism**: Checks `ido52_no_strength_reduce` flag before calling `func_47fbbc`.
If set, skips the call and jumps to `L481000` (continue iteration).

### CRITICAL FINDING: ugen performs independent strength reduction

**The primary stride optimization for `voice*168` functions is in `ugen` (the unrecompiled
code generator), NOT in `f_eliminduct`.** Investigation on 2026-04-02 proved:

1. At `-O2` with `f_eliminduct` skipped, `ugen` STILL emits `addu $2, $2, 168` (stride
   accumulator) instead of `mul reg, reg, 168` per loop iteration.
2. At `-O1`, `ugen` emits `mul reg, reg, 168` pseudo-instructions, which `as1` then
   expands to the shift chain `sll/addu/sll/addu/sll` — matching the target.
3. Verified via `cc -S` output: the `.s` file from `ugen` at `-O2` already contains the
   stride, before `as1` ever runs.

**Implication**: No `uopt` hook can prevent the stride optimization for the `voice*168`
family (~12 functions). The optimization is embedded in the unrecompiled `ugen` binary.

### uopt Pass Order (for reference)

The main optimization pipeline in `uopt` calls passes in this order (line numbers are
call sites in the main driver function):

| Line | Function | Purpose |
|------|----------|---------|
| 126085 | `f_controlflow` | Control flow analysis, calls `f_ivfactor` |
| 126122 | `f_loopunroll` | Loop unrolling |
| 126151 | `f_patchvectors` | Bitvector patching |
| 126160 | `f_procinit_regs` | Register allocation initialization |
| 126471 | `f_findinduct` | Find induction variables (analysis only) |
| 126488 | `f_codemotion` | Code motion (LICM) |
| 126643 | `f_eliminduct` | Eliminate/transform induction variables |
| 127188 | `f_opt_saved_regs` | Optimize saved registers |

### Key functions within f_eliminduct

| Function | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `func_47f8a8` | Recursive tree walker, tags expressions as strength-reducible (sets field `+5` to `5`) |
| `func_47fbbc` | **Actual strength reduction replacement** — resets bitvector bits, changes opcodes |
| `func_47f054` | Registers expression as IV candidate |
| `func_47f428` | Applies IV transformation |
| `func_47ec04` | Marks expression operands in bitvectors |
| `func_47f354` | Loop-specific IV processing |

### Safety Notes

- **DO NOT skip `f_eliminduct` entirely** (via early `return`). Later passes depend on
  bitvector state that `f_eliminduct` maintains. Skipping causes segfaults on complex
  functions (confirmed: `spot_light`, `touzyou_fan_disp`).
- **DO NOT skip `f_findinduct`**. It initializes bitvectors used by ALL subsequent passes.
  Skipping causes `uopt: Internal: code: 411` crash.
- The safe hook point is skipping `func_47fbbc` calls only, while letting the rest of
  `f_eliminduct` run its analysis and bitvector maintenance.

### Test Results (2026-04-02)

| Function | Target | Baseline | Hooked | Delta | Notes |
|----------|--------|----------|--------|-------|-------|
| func_800903D0 | 144 | 93 | 93 | 0 | Stride from ugen, not f_eliminduct |
| scroll_poly_disp | 190 | 190 | 190 | 0 | SR already matching at baseline |
| get_fnt | 247 | 248 | 249 | +1 | Minor effect, wrong direction |
| spot_light | 251 | 250 | 250 | 0 | No effect |
| touzyou_fan_disp | 1168 | 1153 | 1153 | 0 | No effect |

**Conclusion**: Near-zero impact on current blocker functions. `f_eliminduct` handles
a secondary form of strength reduction that rarely applies to the unmatchable set.
The hook remains for future use but does not unblock any existing functions.

---

## Restoration Checklist

If `uopt.c` is regenerated, re-implement in this order:

1. **Top of file** (before `static const uint32_t rodata[]`):
   - Forward declarations for `ido52_hooks_initialized`, `ido52_reserve_sregs_mask_hi`, `ido52_init_hooks()`
   - `IDO52_NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS` comment, `ido52_no_deadstore_addrs[]`, helper function `ido52_is_protected_deadstore()`

2. **Before `f_isearchloop`** (~line 94216):
   - CSE mode constants (`IDO52_NO_CSE_OFF` through `IDO52_NO_CSE_TYPES`)
   - All CSE/const/lda static variables

3. **Inside `f_isearchloop`** (after `s0 = MEM_U32(t9 + 0)` loads hash bucket head):
   - CSE hook block that conditionally sets `s0 = 0`

4. **Inside `f_procinit_regs`** (at function exit, after `MEM_U32(a1 + 4) = t9`):
   - RESERVE_SREGS/MAX_SREGS hook that ORs mask into `MEM_U32(0x1001e608 + 4)`

5. **Inside `f_copypropagate`** (after `f_bvectin` returns nonzero, before `MEM_U8(t9 + 9) = zero`):
   - Deadstore protection check using `MEM_U32(s2 + 4)`

6. **Inside `f_eliminduct`** (similar dead-store elimination point):
   - Deadstore protection check using `MEM_U32(s0 + 4)`

7. **Inside `f_constinreg`** (after prologue `sp = sp + ...`, before decision logic):
   - CONST_NOINREG hook checking `a2` against blocked values

8. **Inside `f_ldainreg`** (at function entry, before any logic):
   - LDA_NOINREG hook checking `a0` against blocked classes

9. **At label `L447124` in `f_isearchloop`** (match/no-match convergence point):
   - CSE freshness counter: check reuse count per ichain, force miss if over limit
   - Requires `ido52_cse_track[]` hash map and `ido52_cse_track_get/inc` helpers at top of file

10. **Bottom of file** (after the last recompiled function):
   - `ido52_init_hooks()` function with all env var parsing
   - Must include `IDO52_CSE_MAX_REUSE`, `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_TYPES`, `IDO52_CSE_FRESHNESS_DEBUG`
   - Must include `IDO52_NO_LICM` address list parsing

11. **At LICM hoist-marking function entry** (~line 181865, before prologue `sp = sp + 0xffffffe0`):
   - Inject NO_LICM hook: if `ido52_no_licm == 1`, early return; if mode 2, check address match
   - Requires `ido52_no_licm`, `ido52_no_licm_addrs[]`, `ido52_no_licm_addr_count`, `ido52_licm_debug` at top of file
   - Also requires `IDO52_NO_LICM` and `IDO52_NO_LICM_ADDRS` parsing in `ido52_init_hooks()`
12. **Inside `f_eliminduct`** (~line 185005, before `func_47fbbc(mem, sp, a0, a1)` call):
   - Strength reduction hook: check `ido52_no_strength_reduce`, skip `func_47fbbc` call if set
   - Requires `ido52_no_strength_reduce`, `ido52_strength_reduce_debug` at top of file
   - Requires `IDO52_NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE`, `IDO52_STRENGTH_REDUCE_DEBUG` parsing in `ido52_init_hooks()`

   - NOTE: As of 2026-04-02, hooks 1 (NO_CSE/NO_CSE_OPCODES/CSE_DEBUG), 3 (CONST_NOINREG_VALUES), 4 (LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES), 10 (CSE_MAX_REUSE), 11 (NO_LICM), and 12 (NO_STRENGTH_REDUCE) are PRESENT. Hooks 2 (originally same as 1 — merged), 5 (RESERVE_SREGS), 6 (MAX_SREGS), 7 (NO_DEADSTORE_GLOBALS), 8 (CSE_DEBUG — now restored as part of hook 1), 9 (DEADSTORE_DEBUG) are MISSING and must be restored.

---

## ugen.c Hooks (NEW: 2026-04-04)

**File**: `tools/ido-static-recomp/build/5.2/ugen.c` (also NOT in git, regenerated by make)

**Apply script**: `python3 tools/ido-static-recomp/apply_ugen_hooks.py`

**Rebuild after patching**:
```bash
cd tools/ido-static-recomp
gcc -c -std=c11 -O0 -ggdb3 -fno-strict-aliasing -I. -o build/5.2/ugen.o build/5.2/ugen.c
gcc -std=c11 -O0 -ggdb3 -fno-strict-aliasing -I. -o build/5.2/out/ugen \
    build/5.2/ugen.o build/5.2/version_info.o build/5.2/libc_impl_52.o -lm
```

**WARNING**: Do NOT use `make VERSION=5.2` after patching -- it will regenerate ugen.c
from the MIPS binary, destroying the hooks. Use the manual gcc commands above.

### Hook 13: IDO52_UGEN_MAX_SREGS (s-register count clamping)

**Env var**: `IDO52_UGEN_MAX_SREGS=N` (0-8, default: off)

**What it does**: Clamps the s-register count in ugen's `f_init_regs` function.
The value at memory address `0x10019008` controls how many s-registers (s0-s7, fp)
ugen allocates for callee-saved variables. uopt sets this via PARAMETERS icode,
and f_main initializes it to 8. This hook intercepts right before f_init_regs
reads the value to build the register free list.

**Effect**: With MAX_SREGS=0, no variables get s-registers -- all cross-call values
go to stack. Each s-register adds 2 instructions (save in prologue + restore in
epilogue). Reducing s-regs changes the frame size and register allocation.

**Hook location**: Label `L43a1e8` in f_init_regs, right before `s1 = MEM_U32(0x10019008)`.

**Test results** (2026-04-04):
- sndi (target 263 insns): SREGS=0 gives 250, SREGS=6 gives 261, SREGS=7 gives 264
- grp_table_calc (target 283 insns): SREGS=0 gives 280, SREGS=1 gives 281, SREGS=2 gives 284
- func_8005970C (target 191 insns): SREGS=7 gives 190, SREGS=8 gives 192
- system_config_pad_mode (target 766): SREGS=0 gives 769 (closest, 3 off)
- Each s-register consistently adds exactly 2 instructions (save + restore)

### Hook 14: IDO52_UGEN_NUM_TREGS (temp register count clamping)

**Env var**: `IDO52_UGEN_NUM_TREGS=N` (0-6, default: off)

**What it does**: Clamps the temp register count in ugen's `f_init_regs` function.
The value at `0x10019010` controls how many temp registers (t0-t5 range) are
available. Reducing temps forces stack spills.

**Hook location**: Label `L439fd8` in f_init_regs, right before `s1 = MEM_U32(0x10019010)`.

**Test results**: TREGS=0 adds instructions (stack spills), TREGS=2+ stabilizes.

### Hook 15: IDO52_UGEN_DEBUG (diagnostic output)

**Env var**: `IDO52_UGEN_DEBUG=1`

**What it does**: Prints the original and clamped register counts to stderr for
each function compiled. Useful for understanding what uopt requested vs what
ugen actually uses.

### Hook 16: IDO52_UGEN_TREG_ORDER (temp register allocation order)

**Env var**: `IDO52_UGEN_TREG_ORDER=t6,t7,t8,t9,t0,t1,t2,t3,t4,t5` (comma-separated, default: off)

**What it does**: Controls the order in which temp registers are allocated from the
free list in ugen's `f_init_regs`. The first register in the list is allocated first.

**Default allocation order** (no hook): t6, t7, t8, t9, t0, t1, t2, t3, t4, t5.
This is because ugen's init adds "extra" registers (t6-t9 from a rodata table at
`0x10005424`) before the standard t-regs (t0-t5 from the loop at `0x10019010`).
The free list is FIFO (append_to_list), so first-added = first-allocated.

**Accepted register names**: t0-t9, s0-s8, or raw MIPS register numbers (8=t0, 14=t6, etc.)

**Hook location**: After label `L43a018` in f_init_regs, after the normal t-reg loop.
The hook clears all existing free list entries (both extras and standard t-regs) and
rebuilds the list in the specified order.

**Effect**: Changes which temp register gets assigned to each temporary value. This
directly affects the register encoding in every instruction that uses a temp register.

**Example**:
```bash
# Reverse allocation order (t5 allocated first instead of t6)
IDO52_UGEN_TREG_ORDER=t5,t4,t3,t2,t1,t0,t9,t8,t7,t6

# Only use t0-t3 (forces spills if more temps needed)
IDO52_UGEN_TREG_ORDER=t0,t1,t2,t3

# Match a target that uses t0 where IDO normally uses t6
IDO52_UGEN_TREG_ORDER=t0,t1,t2,t3,t4,t5,t6,t7,t8,t9
```

**Test results** (2026-04-03):
- With `TREG_ORDER=t0`: all temps use only t0 (verified with 10-temp test function)
- With `TREG_ORDER=t0,t1,t2,t3,t4,t5,t6,t7,t8,t9`: sequential t0-first allocation
- With `TREG_ORDER=t5,t4,t3,t2,t1,t0`: reversed order confirmed in output
- No env var = identical output to unpatched binary (transparent when inactive)
- Note: different t-reg order can change instruction COUNT and frame size due to
  changed scheduling opportunities, not just register names

**IMPORTANT**: This does NOT affect s-register allocation. S-regs are controlled by
`f_opt_saved_regs` in uopt.c, not by ugen's free list.

### Hook 17: IDO52_UGEN_SREG_ORDER (placeholder)

**Env var**: `IDO52_UGEN_SREG_ORDER=s0,s2,s1,...` (NOT YET FUNCTIONAL)

**Status**: Placeholder. S-register allocation order is determined by `f_opt_saved_regs`
in uopt.c, not by ugen's descriptor initialization. All s-reg descriptors are cleared
identically at init time, so reordering them in ugen has no effect. A proper s-reg
order hook would need to be implemented in uopt.c instead.

### Important Limitations

1. **Can only clamp DOWN**: The hook reduces register counts, it cannot increase them.
   If uopt decides to use only 3 s-regs, MAX_SREGS=5 has no effect.

2. **Does not control WHICH variables get s-regs**: Only the total count. The register
   allocator in ugen decides which variables to promote based on its own heuristics.
   S-reg allocation ORDER is controlled by uopt's `f_opt_saved_regs`, not ugen.

3. **ugen strength reduction is independent**: The `ugen`/`upas` code generator performs
   its own strength reduction (converting `mul` to stride accumulator `addu`). This
   cannot be hooked from uopt. See Hook 12 for the voice*168 investigation.

4. **TREG_ORDER can change instruction count**: Changing temp register order affects
   scheduling opportunities in ugen, which can change the total instruction count
   and frame size. This means TREG_ORDER is not just a register rename -- it can
   produce structurally different code.

---

## Hook 16: IDO52_CONST_INREG_VALUES — Force Constants INTO Register Allocation (2026-06-11, extended 2026-07-02)

Inverse of Hook 2. `IDO52_CONST_INREG_VALUES=3,96` makes `f_constinreg` return 1
(keep in register) for the listed constant values. Implemented at the same site as
Hook 2 (~192665), with `ido52_const_inreg_values[64]` / `ido52_const_inreg_count`
parsed in `ido52_init_hooks()` right after CONST_NOINREG.

### Occurrence-window syntax (2026-07-02)

Entry format is now `VALUE[:TYPE[:FROM[:TO]]]`:
- `TYPE`: match `f_constinreg`'s `a0` word exactly (observed values: 8 for
  ==/!=-style compare constants, 6 for sltiu-immediate contexts); `-1` = any.
- `FROM`/`TO`: 1-based occurrence window over the *queries* of that VALUE within
  the uopt run — only the FROM-th..TO-th queries are forced. Default 1..INT_MAX
  (= old behavior). `:0:0` = trace-only entry (never forces; useful with
  `IDO52_CONST_INREG_DEBUG=1`, which now prints EVERY query of a listed value
  with its occurrence index `n=` and a `force=` verdict).
- Occurrence counters are global per uopt process: only reliable when the
  translation unit contains a single function (true for overlay per-function
  files). Query indices are deterministic for a fixed source+env but SHIFT when
  the source or other forcing entries change — re-derive with a trace run.
- ⚠️ COMPAT: the pre-2026-07-02 parser reads `9:-1:16:16` as VALUE=9 TYPE=-1
  (window ignored) = force ALL 9-queries, which is usually catastrophic. Do not
  land windowed @CompileEnv entries until the tree's uopt has been rebuilt with
  this hook (`apply_uopt_hooks.py` + recompile uopt).

### Findings from EVENT/func_8014C3A0 (the occurrence-window pilot, 2026-07-02)

- Forcing a single query can create a REGIONAL const web whose def lands
  byte-exactly where the target has it (girl==5 b22: `9:-1:16:16` produced
  target[749] `li a1,9` in the block-entry bne delay slot, single def, hoisted
  above 6 intra-block skip branches).
- Def placement asymmetry: single-use webs get ONE def hoisted to the region
  entry block; 2-use cross-block webs (girl==2 b10 f6==9 + b11 st==9,
  `9:-1:6:6`) get per-use defs PLUS a merge-point def (+1 instruction vs
  target's single hoisted def). No pair of forced queries merges them.
- Some target const webs are NOT reachable via f_constinreg at all: forcing
  ALL 22 queries of value 8 is completely inert, yet the target holds a t4=8
  web with one def in the girl==8 arm-entry delay slot serving 7 uses across
  three arms. Its creation mechanism is still unknown.

**Limitation discovered**: does NOT make `x * 3` emit `multu` by a cached register —
the small-constant multiply is lowered to sll/subu before/independent of the
f_constinreg decision (see BUNKASAI/func_8014B9E0 residual). Candidate use: forcing
constants like 96 into s-registers (BUNKAKEN/func_8013BD84 wants const-96-in-s4) —
untested.

**⚠️ 2026-07-27 correction (batch 15): the limitation above holds ONLY for SIGNED
multiplies.** `x * 3U` (unsigned) takes a lowering path that DOES query
f_constinreg — `IDO52_CONST_INREG_VALUES=3:-1:1:2` + `* 3U` produced `multu` by a
cached register (BUNKASAI/func_8014437C, byte-perfect). The occurrence window is
mandatory: forcing all queries also converts an unrelated `sltiu $at,$v0,3` into
`addiu`+`sltu` (+1 word). `IDO52_CONST_INREG_DEBUG=1` prints per-query lines only
on a direct `cc` invocation (the test harness swallows them). Also confirmed the
same batch: CONST_INREG cracks NON-LOOP multu-by-nonpow2 with mflo use
(ETC/func_8013D2C8, x240 — the filed "caller-threaded LO" blocker was this plus
an unabsorbed head), extending the batch-14 loop-case refutation. Still true:
CONST_INREG is inert for making `mul` take a register operand when the value IS
queried but ugen re-lowers (ETC/func_8013D560, 8 queries traced, all forced,
output bit-identical).

### 2026-07-02 update (EVENT/func_8014C3A0 investigation 2): mechanism SOLVED

The "unknown mechanism" above is now fully explained (and the note corrected):

- The t4=8 web is NOT one def serving 7 uses — the target has FIVE `li t4,8`
  defs ([221],[235],[662],[1085],[1121]): uopt's live-range machinery SPLITS a
  constant's live range into regional ranges, each with its own def at the
  region head (visible as the `>>>active<<<{8|0}` entries at bits
  950/974/975/976/977 in the `IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5` dump).
- Whether each range materializes into an allocatable register or `$at`
  per-use remats is decided by GRAPH COLORING, not f_constinreg (that is why
  forcing was inert — the queries already return 1 natively; the ranges were
  simply losing coloring: `forbidden: [1..13]`).
- The old `IDO52_PRECOLOR_LIVEIN` combo on that function was itself the
  blocker: the pins occupied colors that displaced all five const-8 ranges.
  Unpinned, the web colors naturally (in `ra` vs the target's `t4` — a
  register-field difference for the paint endgame).
- Emission pipeline (traceable with `IDO52_CONSTQ_TRACE=1`): per const USE,
  `f_gen_outparcode` asks `f_constinreg` (eligibility) then `f_inreg`
  (is the value bound to a register in this block per regdata `+0x44`
  tables) → REGOP if bound, else `f_genloadnum` (immediate-fold or `$at`).

### 2026-07-19: CONST_INREG defeats the held-constant COUNT-blocker class

The single-`li` held-constant sub-class was long listed as a CONFIRMED BLOCKER
(BLOCKER-RESEARCH-AGENDA §6a) because "every uopt hook fails" — but that survey
**omitted CONST_INREG itself** (it tested the block-FROM-register + pass-disable
hooks only). `IDO52_CONST_INREG_VALUES=<held values>` forces the hold and
yields the **exact target instruction count** for cheap single-`li` constants
the target keeps in a register but IDO rematerializes per store:

- `EVENT/func_80151714` (holds 1,2,3 across 9 stores): `=1,2,3` → 54→**48
  exact**; then `+patch_stack_complete 24→40 +scheduler_normalizer
  +gen_reg_patches` → 46/48 resolver-verified.
- `EVENT/func_80155CE4` (holds 2,3,4): `=2,3,4` → **36→36 exact** (control
  confirmation on the agenda's own cited "blocker").

**Open residual = def PLACEMENT, not count.** The hook holds the const from
function entry; when the target materializes it LATE/regionally (func_80151714
`li t0,2` at insn 34, inside the `==2` guard block), the entry-def lands wrong,
leaving 1–2 insns. The occurrence-window (`VALUE:-1:FROM:TO`, above) is the
intended fix but a bisection sweep did not land func_80151714's def at the
guard — same open sub-case as the WEB_BIND_BBS "one-xor-node/two-sites" note.
Functions whose held consts are broadly live (hoisted to entry in the target
too) should close fully; late/regional defs may leave a residual.

### 2026-07-20: two BYTE-PERFECT wins + the store-order rule

When the held const IS broadly live, the sub-class closes fully:
- `EVENT/func_801575E4` → **25/25 BYTE-PERFECT**: `=128` (one `0x80` held in `v0`
  across 7 `sb`).
- `EVENT/func_80155CE4` → **36/36 BYTE-PERFECT** (was "36→36 exact" above, now
  resolver-confirmed): `=3,4,2` + one `gen_reg_patches` line (v0/v1/a0 → t6/t7/t8).

**⚠️ Store-order rule.** The hook fixes the hold/count; the *placement* of each
store is decided by C statement order. A **chained assignment `a = b = c = K;`
compiles the stores in REVERSE source order** → a lo16 residual at every store
site even with the hold forced. Write each store as an explicit statement in
**memory-address (target store) order** — this cleared the final 6 lo16 residuals
on func_801575E4.

**Confirmed inert (CONST_INREG does not apply — left blocked):** `func_80154870`
+ `func_80151714` (live-range-split def-placement, open sub-case above);
`func_8013FF34` (`2<<14` folded at the frontend before the hook runs);
`func_80152B94` (`x*const` strength-reduced); `func_80135730` (shift-*value*
remat, not a literal).

## Hook 17: IDO52_UOPT_TRACE — Original uopt debug listing dumps (2026-07-02)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_UOPT_TRACE` | integer trace level | `IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5` |

Sets the ORIGINAL Pascal uopt trace-level global (0x1001ea98, zeroed during
init). The dumps are written to the listing file opened by uopt's
undocumented `-l <file>` flag — **`-l` is REQUIRED or uopt segfaults in
`f_write_char`** (the listing unit at 0x10011fb8 is only opened when the `-l`
option sets flag 0x1001e65c). Run uopt manually:

```bash
IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5 .../out/uopt -v -G 0 -EL -g0 -O2 -l dump.lst in.B out.O -t in.T
```

(`cc -K` keeps the `.B`/`.O` ucode intermediates needed for a manual run.)

Levels (each also prints the flow graph + itab):
| Level | Dump | Contents |
|-------|------|----------|
| 1 | `printtab`/`printitab` | ichain table: every expression/const node (`[val,sub] isconst`, `isop uequ{ic|sub}{ic|sub}`) |
| 2 | `printlinfo` | live info per bit |
| 3 | `printcm` | PRE/codemotion bitvectors per block (cand/insert/delete/antin/antout) |
| 4 | `printscm` | strength-reduction code motion |
| 5 | `printregs` | **web/coloring dump**: `>>>active<<<{ichain|sub} bit color`, `forbidden:[...]`, `:::interfere with:::`, per-bb liveness incl. `needreglod` (def placement points) |
| 6 | (flow graph only) | globalcolor-region graph prints |
| 7 | `printtab` + `printinterproc` | |
| 0x15 | `printhoist` | hoisting decisions |
| 0x19 | `printprecm` | pre-codemotion |

The level-5 dump is the definitive tool for register-allocation residuals:
it shows every web (variables = `isvar M <n>vreg` by stack-home offset,
globals = `isvar S <symno>`, expressions = `isop ...`, constants =
`{value|subtype}`), its bittab index (the PRECOLOR bitpos space), its final
color (-1 = uncolored → `$at`/spill), its forbidden set and interference.

## Hook 2 extension: CONST_NOINREG occurrence windows (2026-07-18)

`IDO52_CONST_NOINREG_VALUES=VALUE[:FROM[:TO]][,...]` — each entry may carry a
1-based occurrence window over f_constinreg queries of VALUE (mirrors the
CONST_INREG window syntax; plain `VALUE` = all occurrences, fully backward
compatible). `IDO52_CONST_INREG_DEBUG=1` prints `[CONST-NOINREG] val= n= a0=
block=` per query. ⚠️ Blocking is FEEDBACK-RENUMBERING: blocking query N
changes how later queries are numbered (blocked sites re-query), so only TAIL
windows (`V:K:BIG`) are stable; map sites first with single-occurrence probes
(`V:N:N` sweep, watch which site changes form). Proven on TEL/func_8013D758:
`5:23:24` reproduces the target's checked/unchecked div structure exactly
(splits the mflo/mfhi-shared div into the $at-form unchecked dual).

## Hook 18: IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB — color-only web pin (2026-07-02)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB` | `REG:BITPOS[,...]`, REG in v0..t9, s0..s8/fp (2026-07-18), or `no` | `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB=t4:950` |
| `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB_POSTONLY` | presence | restrict to post-gc rebind |

s-reg colors are 17..25 (s0=17 … s7=24, s8/fp=25). ⚠️ Pinning a web that the
stock allocator SPILLED does not un-spill it (spill code was already emitted);
pins recolor register webs only.

Like PRECOLOR_LIVEIN but WITHOUT the live-on-entry side effects (no graphhead
regsused/livein-bitvector/hasstore marking) — intended for regional webs
(split const ranges). `no:BITPOS` kills a web's color post-gc.

**Measured limitations (EVENT/func_8014C3A0):**
- Pre-gc pinning of long var webs reshapes split/def decisions globally
  (structure explosion) — same failure mode as LIVEIN pins on hot webs.
- POSTONLY rebinding makes USES reference the new register (f_inreg reads the
  rebound regdata) but does NOT re-emit range defs (def insertion happens
  inside globalcolor when a range is colored) → uses without defs. Only
  useful for diagnosis.
- `no:BITPOS` on a `hasstore` web produces sw/lw stack traffic, not
  recomputes (spill code was already inserted during coloring).
- Register-field mismatches on structurally-matching webs are better left to
  the sanctioned PostProc paint (gen_reg_patches) than fought with pins.

**⚠️ 2026-07-27 correction (batch 15): a color pin CAN create a copy the natural
compile lacks** — contrary to the earlier NAME_ENT-era finding. When the pin
makes the pinned web and a value web non-coalescable, ugen must materialize the
copies: `IDO52_PRECOLOR_WEB=v0:53` (pin the RETURN web) forced the two missing
`move v0,tX` return copies and byte-perfected SHUGAKU/func_80138E54 (the filed
"no-call phi_copy" wall). Sweep bitpos WIDE (0..60+, not 0..45) — only positions
23 and 53 moved the needle, and the earlier narrow sweep missed both. Triage
first with `IDO52_DUMP_WEBS=1`: if the value never becomes a uopt web (stays a
ugen expression temp, e.g. ETC/func_80144D7C's call-arg `andi`), NO pin can
reach it.

**⚠️ 2026-07-27 second correction (batch 16): that no-web class is deleted by
as1, NOT decided by ugen.** `cc -S` shows ugen already emits the copy; as1's
per-block peephole (`f_do_passII_opt`) renames it away. The working lever is
**`IDO52_AS1_NO_COPY_PROP=all|i,j,...`** (as1 Hook 6 in `apply_as1_hooks.py`;
0-based occurrence indices — a bare numeric is an INDEX, not "all";
`IDO52_AS1_COPY_PROP_TRACE=1` lists candidates as `#N insn=<word>`). Built,
rebuilt into the toolchain 2026-07-27, regression-proven bit-identical when
unset. First win GEKO/func_80133024. The ugen Hook 22
(`IDO52_UGEN_NO_ARG_COALESCE`) exists and functions but is not the fix for this
class. Related unbuilt spec: `IDO52_AS1_NO_SCHED_LOAD_HOIST` (intra-block
load-over-ready-store hoist starving branch delay slots, +1-word epilogue-dup —
3 specimens, MATCHING-TRICKS §Batch 16).

## Hook 19: IDO52_WEB_BIND_BBS — post-gc per-block binding extension (2026-07-02)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_WEB_BIND_BBS` | `BITPOS:BB[:BB...][,...]` | `IDO52_WEB_BIND_BBS=952:402` |

After globalcolor, binds a web's ichain to its ASSIGNED color in the regdata
(`node+0x44`) table of the listed EXTRA basic blocks, so reemission's
`f_inreg` reports the value register-resident there → const compare uses emit
the REGOP form (`xor rX,rC,rV; sltiu`) instead of the immediate fold
(`xori rX,rV,imm`). Emits NO defs and NO liveness/interference changes; a slot
already bound to another ichain is refused with an `OCCUPIED` warning (the
physical register must genuinely still hold the value — the web's last use
must dominate the target bbs with no intervening def of that color, which the
occupied-slot check plus byte-compare against the target verify in practice).

Workflow (EVENT/func_8014C3A0 b38 case study — the `(g==12)` triple):
1. `IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5` printregs dump → find the const web
   (`>>>active<<<{12|0} BITPOS COLOR`) and its `- live bb -` list; note the
   bb where it dies and the bb numbers of the wanted extra uses.
2. `IDO52_CONSTQ_TRACE=1 IDO52_CONST_INREG_DEBUG=1` with a `VAL:-1:0:0`
   trace entry → correlate `[CONST-INREG] n=` occurrence indices with `[Q1]`
   emission decisions; a `cir=0 -> loadnum` at the wanted site needs a
   CONST_INREG occurrence window (`VAL:-1:N:N`) too, because cir=0
   short-circuits before f_inreg is ever consulted.
3. Set both env vars in `@CompileEnv`. Re-derive BITPOS/BB/N after ANY source
   or env change (all three index spaces shift — same caveat as Hook 16
   windows).

Measured results/limitations (EVENT/func_8014C3A0):
- arm3 `(12 ^ g) == 0` (fresh xor node in the bound bb): emits
  `xor v0,t4,v1` = the target's reg-form, byte-exact modulo register paint.
- A SUB spelling (`(g - 12) == 0`) under a forced binding emits `subu` (its
  ucode node is a SUB — the binding controls reg-vs-imm, NOT the operator).
- Does NOT defeat codemotion availability: if the same node was computed in
  an earlier bb and kept available (trace-5 `ppin` lists), the later site
  reuses the temp instead of recomputing — binding the const there changes
  nothing (the one-xor-node/two-sites case remains open).

---

## Hook 20: IDO52_NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT — keep a loop's `<` exit test (2026-07-26)

| Variable | Format | Example |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `IDO52_NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT` | `1` / `all`, or `bb:N[,N…]`, or `occ:N[,N…]` | `IDO52_NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT=1` |
| `IDO52_LOOP_TEST_REPL_DEBUG` | any value (presence enables) | `IDO52_LOOP_TEST_REPL_DEBUG=1` |

### What it does

IDO 5.2 rewrites a countable loop's **relational** exit test (`<` / `<=`,
emitted as `slti/sltu $at,x,LIM` + `bne $at,$zero` = **2 words**) into an
**equality** test (`bne x,lim` = **1 word**) whenever it can prove the
induction variable hits the limit exactly. The 1995 target binary keeps the
relational form on essentially every such loop, so every converted site is a
one-word deficit that no count-neutral PostProc can repair. This hook
suppresses the conversion; the loop's original condition survives untouched.

Default OFF. Unset, the compiler is bit-identical to the unhooked build
(verified: 400 overlay `.c` files compiled with and without the hook code
present produce 400/400 byte-identical `.o`).

### Where it's hooked

Two passes create equality tests; both are gated by the same variable and
share one occurrence counter.

**Primary site — `f_loopunroll` (this is the one that matters).**
Before unrolling, `f_loopunroll` *normalizes* a countable loop's exit test:
four call sites compute the exact trip count and call
`f_change_to_const_eq(mem, sp, a0, a1, a2, a3)` (constant limit) or
`f_change_to_var_eq(...)` (variable limit), store the new condition into
`MEM_U32(s3 + 4)`, then call `f_del_orig_cond` to delete the original.
The hook injects, immediately before each call:

```c
if (ido52_ltr_suppress((uint32_t)MEM_U16(s2 + 8))) goto L47745c;   /* const-eq arms */
if (ido52_ltr_suppress((uint32_t)MEM_U16(s2 + 8))) goto L477508;   /* var-eq arm   */
```

`L47745c` / `L477508` are the state-reload blocks every arm already falls
through to, so the jump skips the conversion *and* its matching
`f_del_orig_cond` and nothing else. `MEM_U16(s2 + 8)` is the basic-block
number — the same value the original listing prints as `EQ_INEQ at BB: n`.

⚠️ **This normalization runs even with `-Wo,-loopunroll,0`.** That is why
years of probing every `-Wo` flag and every pass-disable hook came back inert,
and why the blocker was mis-attributed to `f_eliminduct` / `f_ivfactor`.
`IDO52_SKIP_PASSES=loopunroll` did *not* reach it either — that token was
parsed but never wired to an injection site. **All four dead tokens are wired
as of 2026-07-26** (see Hook 15 below); `loopunroll` now genuinely skips the
pass, which suppresses this normalization as a side effect. Prefer
`IDO52_NO_LOOP_TEST_REPLACEMENT` anyway: it suppresses *only* the exit-test
rewrite, where skipping the whole pass also discards unrolling and the
induction-variable preparation that later passes consume.

**Secondary site — `f_constarith` (the `EQ_INEQ` diagnostic).**
Two stores of the relation code `2` into `MEM_U8(a1 + 5)`, just before the
`EQ_INEQ at BB:` listing line, are guarded the same way. In practice this
path is reached far less often than the `f_loopunroll` one; it is hooked for
completeness.

### Modes

| Value | Behaviour |
|-------|-----------|
| unset / `0` | no effect (default) |
| `1`, `all`, any other bare value | suppress every conversion |
| `bb:4,7` | suppress only in basic blocks 4 and 7 |
| `occ:0,2` | suppress the 0-based 1st and 3rd conversion in the file |

The occurrence counter is **global per uopt run** and advances for every
candidate site whether or not it is suppressed, so `occ:` indices are stable
under different `bb:`/`occ:` selections — but they shift if the source or any
other flag changes. Keep one function per translation unit when using `occ:`.
`IDO52_LOOP_TEST_REPL_DEBUG=1` prints `occ=<n> bb=<n> -> SUPPRESSED|kept` per
candidate, which is how you pick the indices.

### Finding the sites in a target

The original uopt listing names the transform. Run uopt by hand (`cc -K` keeps
the `.B`/`.T` ucode; `-l` is required or uopt segfaults in `f_write_char`):

```bash
IDO52_UOPT_TRACE=5 .../out/uopt -v -G 0 -EL -g0 -O2 -l dump.lst in.B out.O -t in.T
grep -E "EQ_INEQ|numtstrep" dump.lst
```

`EQ_INEQ at BB: n` gives the block; `numtstrep=` counts test replacements.

### Measured results (TAIIKU sweep effect family)

| Function | before | with hook |
|----------|--------|-----------|
| `func_8013EE38` | 440/447 insns, 3.4% exact, ~21% mnemonic | **447/447 count-exact**, 22.4% exact, ~69% mnemonic |
| `func_8013E3D4` | 422/422 "exact" (4 stolen words were masking 4 surplus words), 10.9% exact, ~11% mnemonic | delta −1, 24.2% exact, ~82% mnemonic |
| `func_8013EA6C` | 74.9% exact, delta −2 | delta +1 (paint list is tuned to the unhooked build; left unhooked) |

A count-exact reading on a function with this blocker is **not** evidence the
C is right: on `func_8013E3D4` the four stolen words were exactly cancelling
four surplus words elsewhere. Re-check the count with the hook ON before
trusting it.

Both remaining TAIIKU residuals are the *same* register-allocation shape, and
neither is a compiler blocker class: a walk's limit `&X[4]` (which aliases
`&Y[0]` when the two arrays are adjacent) gets CSE'd into an extra
callee-saved copy (`move s8,sN`) where the target rematerializes
`addiu $sN,$sp,K` inside the loop. `IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1` (sp-relative
LDA) forces the rematerialization but de-registers *all* walk limits (+9 words
on `func_8013EE38`).

### ⚠️ 2026-08-02 CORRECTION — the web split IS expressible (GEKO/func_80142BCC)

The "cannot express a web SPLIT" doctrine below is **disproven in at least
one case**: GEKO/func_80142BCC (914 insns) went BYTE-PERFECT with
`IDO52_LDA_NOINREG_CLASSES=1:14:14,1:15:15` — the tail block rematerializes
the c52 stack base (`addiu $t6,$sp,52` + three folded `addiu $t7,$sp,65`)
while BOTH loops keep the promoted register form of the SAME address.
Mechanism: `f_ldainreg` has two query phases exactly like `f_constinreg`
(the Hook 24 discovery) — **formation** (`a2=3` in the debug trace) and
**emission** (`a2=1`). A window that lands on emission-phase queries flips
individual USE SITES to recompute without dissolving the web. Practical
notes: (a) occurrence numbering is NOT stable once a block takes effect
(blocked sites re-query and reshape the stream — the Hook 3 stability claim
holds only for no-op probes), so sweep singletons empirically and then try
ADJACENT COMBINATIONS; (b) the 2026-07-26 TAIIKU sweep below tried
singletons, prefixes (`1:1:K`), tails (`1:K:99`) and whole-address groups,
but **never mid-range windows** (`1:14:15`-shaped) and never grouped by the
`a2` phase context — both TAIIKU `lda_web_split_remat` filings deserve a
re-attack with emission-phase mid-windows before the category is trusted
again. The section below is kept as measured history.

### ⛔ The LDA occurrence window does NOT close them (measured 2026-07-26)

The occurrence window predicted here was built (Hook 3, above) and swept
exhaustively against both functions. **It is the wrong primitive and the
prediction is retracted.** Full sweep results — honest counts, taken from
`objdump` on the compiled `.o` with the four `types.h` thunks stripped, *not*
from `test_overlay_match.py` (see the count warning below):

| | `func_8013EE38` | `func_8013E3D4` |
|---|---|---|
| baseline (hook off) | **+1**, 54.8% mnemonic | **−1**, 65.6% mnemonic |
| every singleton `1:N:N`, N = 1..28 | no change at all | no change at all |
| every prefix `1:1:K` / tail `1:K:99` | +2 … +11 | +0 … +2, mnemonic collapses to 20–28% |
| whole address group (`a1` grouping) | +3 / +6 | +0 / +3 |
| un-windowed `CLASSES=1` | +9 | +0, 20.4% mnemonic |

Two windows do reach delta 0 on `func_8013E3D4` (`1:1:5` … `1:1:23`, and the
whole-class form) — and every one of them **drops mnemonic identity from 65.6%
to ~22%**. That is the count-exact trap from the top of this section wearing a
different hat: the hook removes one surplus word by restructuring a dozen
others. Do not record any of these as progress.

**Why it cannot work.** `f_ldainreg` is a per-address in-register/not-in-register
predicate. The target's shape is a *split* of one address's live range —
`func_8013EE38`'s target keeps two callee-saved copies of `$sp+172`
(`or s7,s4,zero` / `or fp,s4,zero`) and rematerializes the third use as
`addiu s1,sp,172` inside the loop; this build makes three copies
(`move s6,s3` / `move s7,s3` / `move s8,s3`) and no remat. Blocking the address
kills *all* copies; not blocking it keeps all three. There is no window in
between, at any granularity, because the promotion bit is per-address.

**Levers additionally probed and refuted (2026-07-26):** `SKIP_PASSES=`
`opt_saved_regs` (−17/−18 — it is the save/restore *emission* pass; it deletes
the `sw sN`/`lw sN` block and leaves all three `move`s in place),
`tail_recursion` and `loopunroll` (inert; both functions already carry
`-Wo,-loopunroll,0`), `codemotion` (−53), `eliminduct` (+13/+4),
`copypropagate` and `findinduct` (uopt Internal error 411).

**Correct filing.** The right primitive is a live-range split / rematerialization
control (a "recompute this address at use site N" hook acting on the coloring
pass, not on `f_ldainreg`), which does not exist. Until it does, both functions
should be filed in `config/c_unmatchable.txt` under a new
`lda_web_split_remat` blocker category — *not* under `limit_lda_cse`, which
implied a CSE-suppression fix that has now been shown not to exist. They remain
honest, faithful, near-count SEMIs; leave the C as it is.

### ⚠️ Count warning for these two functions

`test_overlay_match.py` reports both as "447/447" and "422/422" insns, and
`resolve_overlay_relocs.py` reports `len t=1788 c=1788` / `len t=1688 c=1688`.
Both are clipping the compiled buffer to the symbol size. The honest deltas are
**+1** and **−1**. When a function is within a word or two of the target, count
it yourself from `objdump` on the `.o` (strip the leading `jr ra; nop` thunk
pairs) before believing an equal-length report.
